Tropical symplectic flag varieties: a Lie-theoretic approach
George Balla, Xin Fang

Abstract
. We study tropicalization of symplectic flag varieties with respect to the Plücker embedding. We identify a particular maximal prime cone in this tropicalization by explicitly giving its facets. For every interior point of this maximal cone, the corresponding Gröbner degeneration is the toric variety associated to the Feigin-Fourier-Littelmann-Vinberg (FFLV) polytope. Our main tool is the notion of birational sequences introduced by Fourier, Littelmann and the second author, which bridges between weighted PBW filtrations of representations of symplectic Lie algebras and degree functions on defining ideals of symplectic flag varieties. Key words: Tropicalization; Gröbner degeneration; birational sequences; weighted PBW filtration; symplectic Lie algebras



1.
Introduction

1.1.
Motivation

In the past two decades tropical geometry has received great attention in enumerative geometry, mirror symmetry, Diophantine geometry, optimization, to name but a few. The tropical variety associated to an embedded projective variety is a pure sub-fan of the Gröbner fan of the defining ideal of the projective variety. The study of tropical varieties associated to geometric objects arising from Lie theory, such as Grassmannians and flag varieties, is initiated in the work of Speyer and Sturmfels
[
SS04
]
. In this work, the tropical variety associated to the Grassmannian
\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29042}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84054785
of
\mathchar 28722
-planes in an
\mathchar 29038
-space, with the Plücker embedding, gets a complete description by explicitly writing down defining inequalities of the maximal cones in the tropical variety using labelled trivalent trees. Such a complete description is not known for other Grassmannians.

The study of tropical varieties associated to flag varieties of type
{\tt\mathchar 28993}
, called tropical flag varieties, started from the work of Bossinger, Lambogila, Mincheva and Mohammadi
[
BLMM17
]
. In this work, tropical flag varieties of small rank were computed, and the authors showed existence of certain maximal cones related to certain string polytopes and FFLV polytopes using the method of toric degenerations. An explicit description of these cones by their facets or rays was not known in general.

Motivated by the work of Feigin, Fourier and Littelmann
[
FFL11a
,
FFL11b
]
on PBW filtrations for simple Lie algebras and the work of Fourier, Reineke and the second author
[
FFR16
]
on quantum PBW filtrations, weighted PBW filtrations for Lie algebras of type
{\tt\mathchar 28993}_{\mathchar 29038}
are studied in
[
FFFM19
]
: the cone consisting of such filtrations is shown to be sent bijectively to a maximal prime cone in the tropical flag variety of type
{\tt\mathchar 28993}_{\mathchar 29038}
. As a consequence, explicit facet description of this maximal prime cone was obtained. This result stands at the carrefour of certain directions in representation theory as follows.

(a)

The Gröbner degenerations of the type
{\tt\mathchar 28993}_{\mathchar 29038}
flag variety arising:

-

from points in the relative interior of this maximal prime cone yield the toric variety associated to the FFLV polytope
[
FFFM19
]
;

-

from certain faces of this maximal prime cone are linear degenerate flag varieties introduced in
[
CFF+17
,
CFF+20
]
.

(b)

The cone consisting of weighted PBW filtrations of type
{\tt\mathchar 28993}_{\mathchar 29038}
is closely related to the Auslander-Reiten theory for quiver representations:

-

the facets of this cone correspond to Auslander-Reiten sequences in the category of representations of an equioriented type
{\tt\mathchar 28993}_{\mathchar 29038}
quiver
[
FFR21
]
;

-

the faces of this cone parametrize exact structures on the additive category consisting of finite dimensional representations of an equioriented type
{\tt\mathchar 28993}_{\mathchar 29038}
quiver
[
FG22
]
.

We would like to note that Makhlin has provided in
[
Mak22
]
, a full facet description of another maximal cone of the type
A
n
tropical flag variety using similar methods. This maximal cone also parametrizes Gröbner degenerations of the type
A
n
flag varieties, and in particular, every point in its relative interior corresponds to the toric variety associated with the Gelfand-Tsetlin polytope.

1.2.
Symplectic case

In this paper, we generalize results in
[
FFFM19
]
to the symplectic setting: we study weighted PBW degenerations of symplectic Lie algebras, their representations and corresponding geometry. This allows us to provide an explicit description of a maximal prime cone in the tropical symplectic flag variety. Since the notation has already been heavy, we will work with full symplectic flag varieties to avoid introducing further indices. Similar results hold for partial symplectic flag varieties,
mutatis mutandis
.

We define and treat the full symplectic flag variety
\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
necessarily as the closure of a highest weight orbit for the action of the symplectic Lie group on its irreducible representations (see Definition
2.1
). Consider the
\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038
-dimensional complex vector space
\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}^{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
with a fixed symplectic form
\mathchar 28961
. Then
\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
coincides with the variety parametrizing length–
\mathchar 29038
–flags of isotropic subspaces of
\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}^{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
. The tropical symplectic flag variety
\mathrm{\mathchar 29012\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785
is, up to a lineality space, a sub-fan of the Gröbner fan of the defining ideal of
\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
with respect to the Plücker embedding; it is a pure polyhedral fan of dimension
\mathchar 29038^{\mathchar 28722}
in
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathcal{\mathchar 29008}}
, where
\mathcal{\mathchar 29008}
is the index set of all Plücker coordinates on
\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
(see Definition
3.1
). A cone of
\mathrm{\mathchar 29012\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785
is said to be a
prime cone
if the corresponding initial ideal is prime. The following is one of our main results.

Theorem 1.1

(Theorem
3.2
)
.

There is a maximal prime cone
\mathcal{\mathchar 28995}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
in the tropical symplectic flag variety
\mathrm{\mathchar 29012\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785
, with explicit facet description given in Lemma
6.1
, such that every point in its relative interior provides a Gröbner degeneration of
\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
to the toric variety associated with the symplectic FFLV polytope (see Section
4.1
for the definition).

The proof of Theorem
1.1
above uses the bridge between weighted PBW degeneration of the symplectic Lie algebra and degenerations of the symplectic flag variety coming from certain degree functions on its defining ideal with respect to the Plücker embedding.

The tropical variety associated to a special case of the symplectic flag variety, namely, the tropical symplectic Grassmannian, has been studied by Olarte and the first author in
[
BO21
]
. Precisely, for
\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038
, the symplectic Grassmannian parametrizes
\mathchar 29035
-dimensional isotropic linear subspaces of
\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}^{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
. For
\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
, it has been shown in
loc.cit.
that there is a correspondence between maximal cones of the tropical symplectic Grassmannian and maximal cones of the usual tropical Grassmannian
[
BO21
, Prop. 5.1]
. Therefore, one has a full facet description of maximal cones of the tropical symplectic Grassmannian for this case. Such a description for higher
\mathchar 29035
, and more generally, for arbitrary tropical symplectic flag varieties, was not yet known at the point of writing this paper.

Let
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29031}\mathchar 12349\mathfrak{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29040}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 67273472\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}\delimiter 84054785
be the symplectic Lie algebra over
\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}
,
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29031}\mathchar 12349\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8235}\mathchar 8712\mathfrak{\mathchar 29032}\mathchar 8712\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}
a triangular decomposition of
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29031}
, and
\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}
the corresponding set of positive roots.
We introduce a full-dimensional simplicial cone
\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12818\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}}
. An element
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
induces a Lie algebra filtration on
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}
by assigning for each
\mathchar 28940\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}
, degree
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28940\delimiter 84054785
to a chosen basis element
\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 28940}
of weight
\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28940
.
We denote the associated graded Lie algebra by
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
. Let
\mathchar 28949
denote a dominant integral weight for
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29031}
. For a finite dimensional irreducible representation
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}
of
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29031}
with a highest weight vector
\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28949}
, the filtration on the enveloping algebra
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785
obtained from that on
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}
induces a filtration on
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}
(see Section
4.3
). We denote by
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
, the associated graded space; it is a cyclic
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 84054785
-module with cyclic vector
\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
. The space
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
is said to be a
weighted PBW degeneration
of
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}
. For a multi-exponent
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 28940}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathchar 28940\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}}
, we will denote by
\mathchar 29030^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}}_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
the class of
\mathchar 29030^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}}
in
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 84054785
, where
\mathchar 29030^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 28940\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}}\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 28940}}
with the root vectors
\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 28940}
ordered with respect to a fixed order. Let
\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\delimiter 84054785
denote the set of integral points in the symplectic FFLV polytope. We prove the following result which gives the compatibility of the monomial basis labelled by this polytope with weighted PBW degenerations.

Theorem 1.2

(Theorem
4.11
)
.

For every point
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
, the set
\{\mathchar 29030^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}}_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 8705\mathchar 28951^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}_{\mathchar 28949}\ \delimiter 69640972\ \mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12850\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\delimiter 84054785\}
forms a basis of
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
.

For each
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
, we define the
weighted degenerate symplectic flag variety

\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
as the closure of the highest weight orbit
\overline{\exp\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8705\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 84267779}
in the projective space
\mathbb{\mathchar 29008}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 84054785
. We show in Theorem
5.2
, that the defining ideal of
\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
with respect to the Plücker embedding is the initial ideal of the defining ideal of the symplectic flag variety with respect to a degree function
\mathbf{\mathchar 29047}_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
on the Plücker coordinates. The degree function
\mathbf{\mathchar 29047}_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
induces an injective linear map
\mathchar 29047\mathchar 12346\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}}\mathchar 12833\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathcal{\mathchar 29008}}
(See Section
5.1
). Using this construction, we prove Theorem
1.1
by showing that the image of
\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
under
\mathchar 29047
is exactly the maximal prime cone
\mathcal{\mathchar 28995}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
in the tropical symplectic flag variety
\mathrm{\mathchar 29012\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12818\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathcal{\mathchar 29008}}
.

By setting all entries of the point
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
to 1, our degenerate varieties
\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
coincide the symplectic degenerate flag varieties
\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}^{\mathchar 29025}
studied by Feigin, Finkelberg and Littelmann in
[
FFL14
]
. This way, one also recovers the usual (non-weighted) PBW filtration on the symplectic Lie algebra and corresponding irreducible representations studied by Fourier, Feigin and Littelmann in
[
FFL11b
]
. Just like the degenerate varieties in
loc.cit.
, our degenerate varieties are flat degenerations (Theorem
5.2
and Corollary
5.10
). Furthermore, we formulate and prove Borel-Weil type theorems in this setting as well (Theorems
5.11
and
5.12
).

Although the overall strategy of this paper is similar to that in
[
FFFM19
]
, to deal with the symplectic case, we need to develop certain techniques to get control over the representation theory, geometry and related combinatorics.

(a)

In the symplectic case, the fundamental representations are no longer minuscule, and they are only sub-representations of the exterior powers of the vector representation. We used a different argument in Section
4.4
to overcome the non-minuscule problem.

(b)

Birational sequences introduced in
[
FFL17
]
and the associated valuations are used to determine the defining ideal of the weighted degenerate symplectic flag varieties (Theorem
5.2
).

(c)

We make use of the symplectic PBW-semistandard tableaux introduced in
[
Bal22
]
and prove that they form a linear basis of the homogeneous coordinate ring of weighted degenerate symplectic flag variety with respect to the Plücker embedding.

The point of view of
[
FFFM19
]
and this work is different from that of
[
BEZ21
,
BO21
]
: we treat flag varieties as homogeneous spaces associated to algebraic groups and make use of extra structures inherited from the group, while in
loc.cit.
, the authors adopt and work with the linear algebra description of flag varieties, as flags of subspaces of the underlying vector spaces.

1.3.
Organisation of the paper

Section
2
contains preliminaries on symplectic flag varieties. The main object of study, the tropical symplectic flag variety, is recalled in Section
3
. In Section
4
, we treat weighted PBW degenerations of symplectic Lie algebras and their compatibility with FFLV bases. We deal with geometry of weighted PBW degenerations in section
5
and obtain compatibility with valuations coming from birational sequences. Lastly, in Section
6
, we describe the facets of the maximal prime cone in the tropical symplectic flag variety using tools from the previous sections.

2.
Preliminaries: symplectic flag varieties

For
\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29006}
we denote
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29035\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035\}
.

2.1.
Symplectic flag varieties

Let

\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 12850\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}
, we have:
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8705\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28949}
.

Let
N
denote the simply connected Lie group corresponding to
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}
. Then
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}
is a representation of
N
, hence we have an action of
N
on the projectivization
\mathbb{\mathchar 29008}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}\delimiter 84054785
. Let
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28949}\delimiter 84267779
denote the highest weight line through
\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28949}
in
\mathbb{\mathchar 29008}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}\delimiter 84054785
.

Recall that a weight
\mathchar 28949\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8235\ldots\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28675^{\mathchar 8235}
is called regular, if
\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28720
.

Definition 2.1
.

For a regular weight
\mathchar 28949\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28675^{\mathchar 8235}
, the complete symplectic flag variety is defined to be the orbit closure
\overline{\textsc{N}\mathchar 8705\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28949}\delimiter 84267779}
in
\mathbb{\mathchar 29008}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}\delimiter 84054785
.

The isomorphism type of the projective variety
\overline{\textsc{N}\mathchar 8705\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28949}\delimiter 84267779}
is independent of the choice of a regular weight
\mathchar 28949
: we will denote it simply by
\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
.

2.2.
Plücker embedding of symplectic flag varieties

We consider the fundamental representation
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29035}}
of
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29040}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
with highest weight vector
\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29035}}
. The representation
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 28721}}
is the vector representation
\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}^{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
of
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29040}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
with
\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 28721}

\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29035}}
is a subrepresentation of the
\mathchar 29035

(2.1)

\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29035}}\mathrel{\mathchar 300\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}{\mathchar 4950\displaylimits}^{\mathchar 29035}\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}^{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 24891\quad\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29035}}\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8798\ldots\mathchar 8798\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 314

For

We consider the following embedding of varieties

\mathbb{\mathchar 29008}_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\mathbb{\mathchar 29008}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 28721}}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8706\ldots\mathchar 8706\mathbb{\mathchar 29008}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29038}}\delimiter 84054785\xhookrightarrow{}\mathbb{\mathchar 29008}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 28721}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 8706\ldots\mathchar 8706\mathbb{\mathchar 29008}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29038}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29038}}\xhookrightarrow{}\mathbb{\mathchar 29008}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{U}_{\mathchar 28949}\delimiter 84054785

2.3.
Defining relations

We describe the defining ideal of
\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
with respect to the Plücker embedding. As has been shown by de Concini in
[
Dec79
]
, the defining ideal is generated by two kinds of relations: the usual quadratic Plücker relations, and certain linear relations.

Definition 2.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
denote the ideal in
\mathcal{\mathchar 29011}
generated by the quadratic polynomials
\mathchar 29010_{\textsc{L}\mathchar 24891\textsc{J}}^{\mathchar 29043}
for all possible

\mathchar 29011_{\delimiter 67273472\textsc{I}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\textsc{I}_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785}

The following theorem is proved in

when the linear polynomials
\mathchar 29011_{\delimiter 67273472\textsc{I}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\textsc{I}_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785}

(
[

.

The polynomial ring
\mathcal{\mathchar 29011}

\mathrm{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29031}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{X}_{\textsc{J}}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28961_{\delimiter 69640972\textsc{J}\delimiter 69640972}

3.
Tropical symplectic flag varieties

In this section, we consider the tropicalization of symplectic flag varieties using the defining ideal
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12818\mathcal{\mathchar 29011}
(according to Theorem
2.4
). The building blocks for tropical symplectic flag varieties, namely the tropical symplectic Grassmannians, have been studied by the second author and Olarte in
[
BO21
]
, in the spirit of Speyer and Sturmfels’ work on the tropical Grassmannian
[
SS04
]
.

In
[
BO21
]
, it was shown that there are five equivalent characterizations of sympelctic Grassmannians, that turn out not to be equivalent tropically. This makes tropicalization in the symplectic setting subtle, nonetheless, a complete characterization was given. For the current paper, we choose the most natural construction out of the five characterizations for the tropical symplectic flag variety case, namely, the one given by taking the tropical variety of the tropicalization of the ideal
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
, and we seek to understand its fan structure. For a comprehensive introduction to tropical geometry, we refer the reader to
[
MS15
]
.

Consider the Plücker embedding of
\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
in (
2.2
):

\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\mathrel{\mathchar 300\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}{}\mathbb{\mathchar 29008}\Big\delimiter 67273472{\mathchar 4950\displaylimits}^{\mathchar 28721}\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}^{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\Big\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8706\ldots\mathchar 8706\mathbb{\mathchar 29008}\Big\delimiter 67273472{\mathchar 4950\displaylimits}^{\mathchar 29038}\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}^{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\Big\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

For a polynomial

\mathchar 29030\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29006}^{\mathcal{\mathchar 29008}}}\mathchar 28949_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29045}}\mathchar 29016^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29045}}\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29011}\mathchar 24891

its initial form with respect to
\mathbf{\delimiter 69640972}\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathcal{\mathchar 29008}}
is defined to be

\mathrm{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038}_{\mathbf{\delimiter 69640972}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29030\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 8705\mathbf{\delimiter 69640972}\text{ minimal}}\mathchar 28949_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29045}}\mathchar 29016^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29045}}\mathchar 314

The initial ideal of
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
with respect to
\mathbf{\delimiter 69640972}\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathcal{\mathchar 29008}}
is defined as the ideal generated by the initial forms of all polynomial in
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
with respect to
\mathbf{\delimiter 69640972}
:

\mathrm{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038}_{\mathbf{\delimiter 69640972}}\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\delimiter 69632778\mathrm{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038}_{\mathbf{\delimiter 69640972}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29030\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29030\mathchar 12850\mathfrak{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 86414091\mathchar 12818\mathcal{\mathchar 29011}\mathchar 314

From the above construction, it follows that every point
\mathbf{\delimiter 69640972}\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathcal{\mathchar 29008}}
defines a Gröbner degeneration of
\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
. Consider an equivalence relation on
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathcal{\mathchar 29008}}
given by setting
\mathbf{\delimiter 69640972}\mathchar 12824\mathbf{\delimiter 69640972}^{\mathchar 560}
whenever

\mathrm{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038}_{\mathbf{\delimiter 69640972}}\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathrm{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038}_{\mathbf{\delimiter 69640972}^{\mathchar 560}}\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

Each equivalence class corresponds to points in the relative interior of a convex rational polyhedral cone in
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathcal{\mathchar 29008}}
. The collection of all such cones defines the Gröbner fan of
\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
. We are interested in the following subfan of this Gröbner fan.

Definition 3.1
.

The tropical symplectic flag variety with respect to the Plücker embedding is defined by

\mathrm{\mathchar 29012\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\{\mathbf{\delimiter 69640972}\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathcal{\mathchar 29008}}\mathchar 12906\mathrm{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038}_{\mathbf{\delimiter 69640972}}\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785\text{ contains no monomials}\mathchar 24891\ \mathbf{\delimiter 69640972}\delimiter 67273472\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29028\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\}\mathchar 314

The normalization
\mathbf{\delimiter 69640972}\delimiter 67273472\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29028\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
is chosen as a special section of the quotient map according to the multi-homogeneous property of the Plücker embedding.

By the Bieri-Groves theorem (
[
BG84
]
),
\mathrm{\mathchar 29012\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785
is a pure polyhedral fan in
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathcal{\mathchar 29008}}
of dimension
\mathchar 29038^{\mathchar 28722}
. For example,
\mathrm{\mathchar 29012\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28724}\delimiter 84054785
is a pure
\mathchar 28724
-dimensional fan in
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720}
with a
\mathchar 28722
-dimensional lineality space having
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720
rays and
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28725
maximal cones (see
[
BO21
, Example 9.4]
).

The following notion is introduced in
[
BLMM17
]
: a maximal cone in
\mathrm{\mathchar 29012\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785
is called
prime
, if the initial ideal associated to a point (hence any point) in its relative interior is a prime ideal.

Giving explicit descriptions of all polyhedral cones in
\mathrm{\mathchar 29012\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785
seems to be only possible when
\mathchar 29038
is very small. One of the goals of this paper is to prove the following theorem that generalizes results in the same direction for type
{\tt\mathchar 28993}
flag varieties in
[
FFFM19
]
.

Theorem 3.2
.

There is a maximal cone
\mathcal{\mathchar 28995}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
in the tropical symplectic flag variety
\mathrm{\mathchar 29012\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785
:

(1)

that is a prime cone with explicit facet description given in Lemma
6.1
, and

(2)

such that every point in its relative interior provides a Gröbner degeneration of
\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
to the toric variety associated with the symplectic FFLV polytope (see Section
4.1
for the definition of this polytope).

The proof of Theorem
3.2
(1) will be given in Section
6
, using weighted PBW degenerations of irreducible representations of the symplectic Lie algebra constructed in Section
4
and their bridge to valuations coming from birational sequences described in Section
5
. Part (2) of this theorem is proved in Section
5.5
.

Remark 3.3
.

For type
{\tt\mathchar 28993}
flag varieties, such a maximal cone stands at the carrefour of tropical geometry, Lie theory (
[
FFL11a
,
Fei12
,
FeFL17
,
FFFM19
]
), geometry of quiver representations (
[
CFF+17
,
CFF+20
]
), an exact category and Hall algebras (
[
FG22
]
). The connection between the maximal cone described in the current paper and the work of Boos and Cerulli Irelli
[
BCI21
]
is not yet clear.

4.
Weighted PBW degenerations and compatible bases

The PBW degenerations of the symplectic Lie algebras and their representations are studied in
[
FFL11b
]
. They proved that the symplectic FFLV basis (see Section
4.1
below) is compatible with the filtration induced to the irreducible representations from the PBW filtration on the universal enveloping algebra
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785
. The goal of this section is to show that this basis is also compatible with a generalization of the PBW filtration that we consider.

4.1.
The symplectic FFLV basis

We follow
[
FFL11b
]
to describe a basis of the irreducible representation
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}
parametrized by points in the FFLV polytope.

We first recall the notion of a symplectic Dyck path.

A
symplectic Dyck path
is a sequence
\mathsf{\mathchar 29040}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29035\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
,
\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28720
, of positive roots in
\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}
satisfying the following conditions:

(i)

the first root
\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29033}
for some
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038
,
i.e.
it is simple;

(ii)

the last root
\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29035\delimiter 84054785
is either a simple root
\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29035\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29036}
for some
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29036\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038
or
\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29035\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\overline{\mathchar 29034}}
for some
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12604\mathchar 29038
;

(iii)

the elements in between satisfy the recursion rule: If
\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29040\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29041}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}

or

denotes the smallest element in

which is bigger than
\mathchar 29041
.

Denote by
\mathbb{\mathchar 28996}
the set of all symplectic Dyck paths. For a dominant, integral weight

and for all

(4.1)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}&\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 8235\ldots\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29035\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8235\ldots\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 24891\quad\text{if}\quad\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 24891\quad\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29035\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 24891\\

Theorem 4.1

(

.

For any dominant integral weights

\mathrm{\mathchar 28998\mathchar 28998\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29014}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28950\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathrm{\mathchar 28998\mathchar 28998\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29014}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8235\mathrm{\mathchar 28998\mathchar 28998\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29014}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28950\delimiter 84054785\quad\text{and}\quad\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28950\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8235\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28950\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24635

The Minkowski sum property
\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28950\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8235\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28950\delimiter 84054785

and
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12850\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28950\delimiter 84054785

\mathchar 28949\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8235\ldots\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29038}

into a sum of elements in

\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8235\ldots\mathchar 8235\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8235\ldots\mathchar 8235\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8235\ldots\mathchar 8235\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785

where

appears
\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29035}
times. Such a definition is given in an inductive manner on the height
\mathrm{\mathchar 29032\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8235\ldots\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29038}
of

. Let
\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\max\{\mathchar 352\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 352}\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 28720\}

\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8235\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}_{\mathchar 28722}
with
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12850\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785
and
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12850\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785
.

For
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 28940}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29006}^{\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}}
we denote
\mathrm{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29045\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29040}\,\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\{\mathchar 28940\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 28940}\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 28720\}
to be the support of
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}
. The set of positive roots admits a poset structure by defining
\mathchar 28940_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12831\mathchar 28940_{\mathchar 28722}
if there exists a symplectic Dyck path from
\mathchar 28940_{\mathchar 28721}
to
\mathchar 28940_{\mathchar 28722}
. Let
\mathchar 28680_{\mathchar 29035}^{\mathchar 8235}\mathchar 12818\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}
be the set of positive roots
\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}
satisfying
\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29034
. We denote by
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 28680_{\mathchar 29035}^{\mathchar 8235}}\mathchar 12818\mathbb{\mathchar 29006}^{\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}}
to be the restriction of
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}
to
\mathchar 28680_{\mathchar 29035}^{\mathchar 8235}
and then extended by zero to the entire
\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}
. We then define
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29006}^{\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}}
to be the characteristic function of the set of maximal elements (with respect to the above partial order) in
\mathrm{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29045\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29040}\,\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 28680_{\mathchar 29035}^{\mathchar 8235}}\delimiter 84054785
.

Lemma 4.2
.

We have
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12850\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785
and
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12850\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785
.

Proof.

By
[
ABS11
]
, the FFLV polytope
\mathrm{\mathchar 28998\mathchar 28998\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29014}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\delimiter 84054785
is a marked chain polytope associated to the poset structure on
\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}
defined above with marking given by
\mathchar 28949
. When
\mathchar 28949\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29035}
is a fundamental weight,
\mathrm{\mathchar 28998\mathchar 28998\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29014}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785
is supported on
\mathchar 28680_{\mathchar 29035}^{\mathchar 8235}
; when considered inside
\mathbb{\mathchar 29006}^{\mathchar 28680_{\mathchar 29035}^{\mathchar 8235}}
, it is the chain polytope associated to the induced poset
\mathchar 28680_{\mathchar 29035}^{\mathchar 8235}
. The lattice points in the chain polytope have a bijection to anti-chains in the poset
[
Sta86
]
. The maximal element of a subset of
\mathchar 28680_{\mathchar 29035}^{\mathchar 8235}
is an anti-chain, hence
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12850\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785
.

Since a Dyck path intersects an anti-chain at at most one element,
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}_{\mathchar 28721}
satisfies all defining inequalities of
\mathrm{\mathchar 28998\mathchar 28998\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29014}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785
, hence it is contained in
\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785
.
∎

4.2.
Symplectic FFLV degree cone

Keep the notations in Section
2.1
. We define a polyhedral cone whose points will be used to construct a PBW type like filtrations on symplectic Lie algebras and their representations.

The vector space
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}}
consists of functions from
\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}
to
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}
. For a point
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}}
, we will simply write
\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785
for
\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}
. Notice that in our notation there is no difference between
\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038}
and
\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\overline{\mathchar 29038}}

Definition 4.3
.

The symplectic FFLV degree cone for
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29040}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
is the polyhedral cone
\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12818\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}}
defined by the following inequalities:

(A

)

\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12821\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\qquad\qquad\ \text{for}\ \mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721

(B

\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\overline{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\overline{\mathchar 29034}}\mathchar 12821\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\overline{\mathchar 29034}}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\overline{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 24891\ \ \text{for}\ \mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12604\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721

(D

\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\overline{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 12821\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\overline{\mathchar 29033}}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\overline{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 24891\qquad\qquad\text{for}\ \mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721

That is to say,

The polyhedral geometric properties of
\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
are summarized in the following proposition:

Proposition 4.4
.

(1)

The cone
\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
is full dimensional in
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}}
.

(2)

The defining inequalities of
\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
give the complete set of its facets.

(3)

The cone
\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
has a lineality space of dimension
\mathchar 29038
.

(4)

The cone
\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
is simplicial.

Proof.

We start with showing the full-dimensionality of
\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
by constructing a solution to the strict inequality system in the definition of
\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
. First set

for

for

are bounded above: choose a feasible solution for these coordinates. Then we look at the coordinates

\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 24891\ \mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28724}\mathchar 24891\ \mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28724}\mathchar 24891\ \ldots\mathchar 24891\ \mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 24891\ \ldots\mathchar 24891\ \mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038}

in this order. By (

\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\overline{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 24891\ \ldots\mathchar 24891\ \mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\overline{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 24891\ \ldots\mathchar 24891\ \mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\overline{\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 24891\ \mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\overline{\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 24891\ \mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\overline{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 12346

the same argument as above using (

) and (
\mathrm{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 29033}
) gives a point satisfying all strict inequalities. That is to say, we obtain a point in

We prove the other statements simultaneously. Let

\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\overline{\mathchar 29034}}\mathchar 8704\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8235\ldots\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 8235\ldots\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\ \ \mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721

Remark 4.6
.

Since in the proof we only sum up inequalities from the defining facets of
\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
, if
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}

, all inequalities in Proposition

are strict.

Proposition 4.7
.

For
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
, the subspaces
\{\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}_{\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}\}
define an
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}
-filtration of Lie algebra on
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}
; the subspaces
\{\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}_{\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}\}

Proof.

We need to verify that

\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 28939}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 28940}\delimiter 84267779

\mathchar 28939\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28940\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}

(1)

for
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
and
\mathchar 29034\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12820\overline{\mathchar 29033}
,
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84267779
is a non-zero scalar multiple of
\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}

(2)

for

with
\mathchar 29033\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 29034\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721
,
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 29035\mathchar 24891\overline{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}}\delimiter 84267779
is a non-zero scalar multiple of
\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\overline{\mathchar 29035}}

Since

, the inequalities (
\mathrm{\mathchar 28993}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}

The statement on
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785
follows from the above argument.
∎

Similarly one defines the subspaces

\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4940\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}}\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}_{\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 24891\ \ \text{where}\ \ \mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}_{\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}_{\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}_{\mathchar 12604\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 314

For a root vector
\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}
with
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038

The following proposition can be looked at as a kind of functoriality of the degeneration from

Proposition 4.8
.

The linear map
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
, sending
\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
to the class of
\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}
in
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
, induces an isomorphism of algebras,
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\textstyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
.

Proof.

From the inequalities (A), (E) and (F) in Proposition
4.5
, there exists a morphism of Lie algebras,
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
. From the universal property, there exists a morphism of algebras,
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
. It is an isomorphism by PBW theorem for
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785
.
∎

From now on we will not distinguish
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 84054785
and
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
.

For a polyhedral cone
\mathchar 28995
, let
\mathrm{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29036\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28995\delimiter 84054785
denote its relative interior.

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 24891\mathbf{\mathchar 29029}\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
be contained in the relative interior of the same face.

(1)

The linear map

\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 12833\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29029}}\mathchar 24891\ \ \delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 28940}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 28940}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29029}}\ \ \text{for }\mathchar 28940\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}

is an isomorphism of Lie algebras.

(2)

The associated graded algebras
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
and
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29029}}
are isomorphic.

Proof.

The part (2) is a direct consequence of (1) and Proposition
4.8
. We prove (1). Recall that whether the Lie bracket
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 28939}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 28940}\delimiter 84267779
for
\mathchar 28939\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28940\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}
in
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}
is zero depends on whether
\mathchar 28939\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28940
is a positive root. Given
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
, the Lie brackets in
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
are determined by whether inequalities
\delimiter 67273472\mathrm{\mathchar 28993}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785
,
\delimiter 67273472\mathrm{\mathchar 28997}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785
and
\delimiter 67273472\mathrm{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785
in Proposition
4.5
are strict or not. Notice that in the proof of the proposition, the above inequalities are obtained from summing up certain defining inequalities of
\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
in Definition
4.3
. From Proposition
4.4
, they are facets of
\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
. Therefore the Lie brackets in
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
will remain the same when
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}
varies in the relative interior of a face.
∎

As a consequence, the isomorphism type of the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
is constant on the relative interior of each face of
\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
.

We have a closer look at
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
and
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
for
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12850\mathrm{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29036\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785
.

Lemma 4.10
.

If
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12850\mathrm{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29036\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785
,
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
is isomorphic to the symmetric algebra
\mathchar 29011\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785
as an algebra.

Proof.

The same argument as in the proof of Lemma
4.9
shows that if all inequalities in Definition
4.3
are strict, then for any
\mathchar 28939\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28940\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}
,
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 28939}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 28940}\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
in
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
, hence
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
is an abelian Lie algebra.
∎

Such a filtration on
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785
induces a filtration on cyclic modules. For any dominant integral weight
\mathchar 28949
, the simple
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29040}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
-module
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8705\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28949}
is cyclic. For a fixed
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
, we consider the induced
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}
-filtration

\delimiter 67273472\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29037}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}_{\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 8705\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28949}

and similarly its subspace
\delimiter 67273472\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathchar 12604\mathchar 29037}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
.
Let us denote the associated graded space by

, i.e.

\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4940\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28720}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 24891\ \ \text{where}\ \ \delimiter 67273472\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}_{\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 68408078\delimiter 67273472\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}_{\mathchar 12604\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 314

The vector space
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
carries naturally a graded
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 84054785
-module structure. Indeed, for any
\mathchar 29035\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29036\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}
, we have by definition

\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 67273472\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29036}\mathchar 12818\delimiter 67273472\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29036}\mathchar 314

The
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 84054785
-module
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
will be termed a

of
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}
.

Let
\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
denote the image of
\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28949}
in
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
. The
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 84054785

We fix
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12850\mathrm{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29036\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785
. For
\mathchar 28949\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28675^{\mathchar 8235}
, since
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
is a cyclic
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
-module, we obtain a surjective
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
-module map

\mathchar 28967_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29011\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12833\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 24891\ \ \mathchar 29048\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8705\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 314

We set
\mathrm{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\ker\mathchar 28967_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
. It is an ideal in
\mathchar 29011\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785
.

Recall that in Section
4.1
we have fixed an enumeration of positive roots
\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}\mathchar 12349\{\mathchar 28940_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28940_{\mathchar 29006}\}
. For a multi-exponent
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 28940}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathchar 28940\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}}
, we will denote by
\mathchar 29030^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}}_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
the class of
\mathchar 29030^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}}
in
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
.

The first main result of the paper is the following compatibility of the FFLV basis and the weighted PBW degenerations.

Theorem 4.11
.

For every point
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
, the set
\{\mathchar 29030^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}}_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 8705\mathchar 28951^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}_{\mathchar 28949}\ \delimiter 69640972\ \mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12850\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\delimiter 84054785\}
forms a basis of
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
.

The rest of this section will be devoted to the proof of the theorem.

4.4.

Lemma 4.12

Proposition 4.13
.

For
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12850\mathrm{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29036\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785

forms a basis for
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 28721}}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
.

Proof.

By
[
FFL11b
, Lemma 4.1]
, non-zero elements in
\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785
are precisely
\{\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 28722\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12820\overline{\mathchar 28721}\}
. Now Lemma
4.12
implies that
\{\mathchar 29030^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}}_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 8705\mathchar 28951^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}_{\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 28721}}\ \delimiter 69640972\ \mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12850\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\}
spans
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 28721}}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
. By Theorem
4.1
,
\delimiter 69640972\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 12349\dim\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 28721}}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
, hence the above generating set forms indeed a basis of
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 28721}}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
.
∎

Lemma 4.14
.

The function
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29028}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
has a unique minimum on
\mathchar 29005_{\mathchar 29001}^{\mathchar 29002}
attained at
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}_{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29002}

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
\mathchar 29035
, the index of the fundamental representation. The starting point

We first show that it suffices to prove the lemma under the assumption

If this intersection is not empty, there exists
\mathchar 29042
and

Proposition 4.15
.

For
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12850\mathrm{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29036\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785

4.4.3.
From interior to boundary

We now prove Theorem

for
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}

\mathchar 28949\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29035}

Proposition 4.16
.

For any
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
, the set
\{\mathchar 29030_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}}\mathchar 8705\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29035}}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 12906\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12850\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785\}

Proof.

When

is chosen from the boundary of the cone

, in the proofs of Lemma

and Lemma

above, the strict inequalities used therein from Proposition

are not necessarily strict, and therefore the proofs imply that the function
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29028}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
attains one of its minimum at
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}_{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29002}
(such a minimum is not necessarily unique). The set
\{\mathchar 29030_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}}\mathchar 8705\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29035}}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 12906\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12850\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785\}

(2).
∎

4.5.
Proof of Theorem
4.11

Let
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
. We proceed the proof by induction on the height
\mathrm{\mathchar 29032\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29035}
of the weight
\mathchar 28949\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29035}
. When

,
\mathchar 28949
is a fundamental weight: such cases are settled in Section
4.4
. For the inductive step we need to describe the Cartan component of the
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 84054785
-module
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 8714\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28950}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
. We start from the following lemma:

Lemma 4.17
.

The set
\{\mathchar 29030^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}}_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 8705\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 8714\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28950}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12906\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12850\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28950\delimiter 84054785\}
is linearly independent in
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 8714\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28950}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
.

Proof.

The proof is the same as
[
FFR16
, Proposition 4]
, we sketch it for the convenience of the reader.

Assume that there exists a non-trivial linear combination

(4.4)

\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12850\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28950\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 29027_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}}\mathchar 29030_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}}\mathchar 8705\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 8714\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28950}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720\mathchar 314

Let
\mathchar 12831^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
be the partial order on
\mathbb{\mathchar 29006}^{\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}}
defined by:
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12831^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}
if and only if
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29028}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12606\mathfrak{\mathchar 29028}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785
. We fix a linearization of this partial order, which is also denoted by
\mathchar 12831^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
.

Let
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\max\{\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\mathchar 12850\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28950\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29027_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}}\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 28720\}
where the maximum is taken with respect to
\mathchar 12831^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
. By induction hypothesis we can write

(4.5)

\mathchar 29030_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}}\mathchar 8705\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 8714\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28950}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\mathchar 12850\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28950\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\mathchar 12850\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 28941_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}}\mathchar 29030_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}}\mathchar 8705\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 8714\mathchar 29030_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}}\mathchar 8705\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28950}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}

with

. We set

. In (

with

∎

Proposition 4.18
.

For
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}

Proof.

\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 84054785

. It suffices to prove that if
\mathchar 29030\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 8704}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 84054785

Now we can complete the proof of Theorem

, the set

4.6.
Monomial ideal

When
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}
is taken from the interior of
\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
, we have the following

Corollary 4.19
.

For
\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}
in the interior of
\mathcal{\mathchar 29003}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
and
\mathchar 28949\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28675^{\mathchar 8235}
, if
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\hfil\textstyle\mkern 1.0mu\delimiter 1299214\hfil$\crcr$\textstyle\mathchar 562$\crcr}}}\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\delimiter 84054785
, then
\mathchar 29030^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}}_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 8705\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
. In particular, the ideal
\mathrm{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
is monomial.

Proof.

We again proceed by induction on the height
\mathrm{\mathchar 29032\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\delimiter 84054785
. When
\mathrm{\mathchar 29032\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
, the corollary is a consequence of Lemma
4.14
.

We consider the weight
\mathchar 28949\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28950
for
\mathchar 28949\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28950\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28675^{\mathchar 8235}
. If
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\hfil\textstyle\mkern 1.0mu\delimiter 1299214\hfil$\crcr$\textstyle\mathchar 562$\crcr}}}\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28950\delimiter 84054785
, for any decomposition
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12349\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8235\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}_{\mathchar 28722}
where
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29006}^{\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}}
, either
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\hfil\textstyle\mkern 1.0mu\delimiter 1299214\hfil$\crcr$\textstyle\mathchar 562$\crcr}}}\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\delimiter 84054785
or
\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}_{\mathchar 28722}\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\hfil\textstyle\mkern 1.0mu\delimiter 1299214\hfil$\crcr$\textstyle\mathchar 562$\crcr}}}\textsc{S}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28950\delimiter 84054785
. It follows by induction that
\mathchar 29030^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}}_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 8705\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 8714\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28950}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
in
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 8714\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28950}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
. By Proposition
4.18
,
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28950}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
is the Cartan component in
\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 8714\textsc{V}_{\mathchar 28950}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
, hence
\mathchar 29030^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}}_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 8705\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28949\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28950}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
.
∎

5.2.

We recall a set of tableaux from

which is compatible with weighted degenerations (Corollary

To a dominant integral weight
\mathchar 28949\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28675^{\mathchar 8235}

\mathchar 29017_{\mathchar 28949}

A symplectic PBW tableau,
\textsc{T}_{\mathchar 28949}
of shape
\mathchar 28949
is a filling of the corresponding Young diagram
\mathchar 29017_{\mathchar 28949}
with numbers
\textsc{T}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12850\{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\overline{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\overline{\mathchar 28721}\}
such that for

, the length of the
\mathchar 29034
-th column, we have:

\textsc{T}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29033
;

(ii)

if

, and
\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12604\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 28722}
, then
\textsc{T}_{\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12606\textsc{T}_{\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}

\mathchar 569\,\,\mathchar 29033^{\mathchar 560}
such that
\textsc{T}_{\mathchar 29033^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12349\overline{\mathchar 29033}

\mathchar 29033^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 12604\mathchar 29033

A symplectic PBW tableau is said to be PBW-semistandard if in addition, the following condition is satisfied:

(iv)

for every
\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28721
and every
\mathchar 29033
,
\mathchar 569\,\,\mathchar 29033^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 12821\mathchar 29033

Theorem 5.4

(
[
Bal22
]
)
.

The elements
\textsc{X}_{\textsc{T}}
,
\textsc{T}\mathchar 12850\textsc{SyST}_{\mathchar 28949}

We will need the following construction in Section
6
.

For
\mathrm{\mathchar 29002}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29034_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034_{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29008}

\mathchar 29033
-th box from top). The assumption ensures
\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12604\mathchar 29040

\mathchar 29040

5.3.
Birational sequences and affine chart

We introduce a chart of
\textsc{Sp}\mathcal{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}^{\mathbf{\mathchar 29028}}
motivated by birational sequences introduced in
[

]
.

We enumerate the positive roots in
\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 8235}\mathchar 12349\{\mathchar 28940_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28940_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28940_{\mathchar 29006}\}
in such a way that if
\mathchar 28940_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28940_{\mathchar 29034}
is a sum of positive roots, then
\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12604\mathchar 29034
. Such a sequence of positive roots
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28940_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28940_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28940_{\mathchar 29006}\delimiter 84054785
is called a

in
loc.cit.
This fixed enumeration gives an isomorphism of affine varieties

.

The map
\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 29035}

Proof.
